[Pressured by US, Brussels pulls out of Disinfo 2026 ...](https://www.intelligenceonline.com/europe-russia/2026/10/07/pressured-by-us-brussels-pulls-out-of-disinfo-2026-conference-in-vilnius,110892477-art)

**English translation**

**In short**

Formally, the EU, Canada, and Lithuania explained their withdrawal of support by stating that the wording of part of the program did not align with their official positions. However, according to Politico data (cited by LRT, CBC, The Globe and Mail), the decisive factor was pressure from the Trump administration: the conference program described the US as a subject of "Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference" (FIMI) — alongside Russia and China.

**What happened**

**The conference.** #Disinfo2026 is an annual conference by EU DisinfoLab (a Brussels-based NGO), held on October 7–8, 2026, in Vilnius. The sessions included, in particular: - "USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back" — about how the US has become a disinformation threat to Europe; - "Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure" — about pressure from US authorities, including entry bans for European experts; - A panel on how the US "acted as a FIMI actor in certain Arctic contexts."

**Washington's reaction.** The State Department sent a cable to US embassies in several European countries and to the EU, asking them to express Washington's concern and to "assess" whether the participating governments agreed with characterizing the US as a FIMI actor. The cable called this a "false equivalence" that "obscures the scale and nature of Russian and Chinese interference."

**Withdrawal of support.** Canada and Lithuania asked the organizers on Monday to remove their ministries' logos from conference materials, and the European External Action Service (EEAS) did so on Tuesday. On the stands, the logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were covered with white stickers; France remained the only major sponsor.

**Official explanations from the parties**

- **EEAS:** The logo was removed because "the organizers decided to present certain discussions in a way that does not correspond to the official positions of the EU."

- **Lithuanian MFA:** "Certain parts of the program did not correspond to the positions of the government and the national interests of Lithuania"; Minister Kęstutis Budrys said that the organizers "went too far and made a huge mistake," and that he is "surprised and shocked" by putting the US in the same category as Russia and China.

- **Global Affairs Canada:** "Following changes in the presentation of several panel discussions," the department reviewed its participation; Canadian official Saliu Babu refused to chair the Arctic session. At the same time, Canada stated that it respects the organizers' right to define the framework of discussions themselves.

- **State Department:** "It should come as no surprise that countries distanced themselves from the conference upon learning that the US was called a 'FIMI threat' at it."

**Context**

According to Intelligence Online, the withdrawal of support was preceded by an investigation by an influential MAGA blogger and social media attacks on the conference. The organizers declined to comment. Participants (for example, American Sunlight Project researcher Ben Schultz) called the simultaneous exit of the three sponsors a "shock" and wondered if it was the result of pressure.

**Bottom line:** Publicly, all three parties cited the inconsistency of the program with their positions, but journalistic investigations indicate that the key reason was a US pressure campaign against characterizing Washington as a source of disinformation.

**Original text**

**Коротко**

Формально ЕС, Канада и Литва объяснили отзыв поддержки тем, что формулировки части программы не совпадали с их официальными позициями. Но, по данным Politico (на которые ссылаются LRT, CBC, The Globe and Mail), решающим фактором было давление администрации Трампа: в программе конференции США описывались как субъект «иностранной манипуляции информацией и вмешательства» (FIMI) — наравне с Россией и Китаем.

**Что произошло**

**Конференция.** #Disinfo2026 — ежегодная конференция EU DisinfoLab (брюссельская НКО), проходила 7–8 октября 2026 года в Вильнюсе. Среди сессий были, в частности: - «USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back» — о том, как США стали угрозой дезинформации для Европы; - «Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure» — о давлении американских властей, включая запреты на въезд для европейских экспертов; - панель о том, что США «в отдельных арктических контекстах действовали как FIMI-актор».

**Реакция Вашингтона.** Госдепартамент разослал телеграмму в посольства США в ряде европейских стран и в ЕС с просьбой выразить обеспокоенность Вашингтона и «оценить», согласны ли правительства-участники с характеристикой США как FIMI-актора. В телеграмме это называлось «ложной эквивалентностью», которая «затуманивает масштаб и характер российского и китайского вмешательства».

**Отзыв поддержки.** Канада и Литва в понедельник попросили организаторов убрать логотипы своих министерств из материалов конференции, Европейская служба внешних действий (EEAS) — во вторник. На стендах логотипы Канады, Литвы и ЕС были заклеены белыми стикерами; единственным крупным спонсором осталась Франция.

**Официальные объяснения сторон**

- **EEAS:** логотип убран, поскольку «организаторы решили подать некоторые дискуссии так, как это не соответствует официальным позициям ЕС».

- **МИД Литвы:** «отдельные части программы не соответствовали позициям правительства и национальным интересам Литвы»; министр Кястутис Будрис сказал, что организаторы «хватили через край и совершили огромную ошибку», и что он «удивлён и шокирован» постановкой США в одну категорию с Россией и Китаем.

- **Global Affairs Canada:** «после изменений в подаче нескольких панельных дискуссий» ведомство пересмотрело участие; канадский чиновник Салю Бабу отказался председательствовать на сессии по Арктике. Канада при этом заявила, что уважает право организаторов самим определять рамки дискуссий.

- **Госдепартамент:** «не должно быть сюрпризом, что страны дистанцировались от конференции, узнав, что на ней США названы "FIMI-угрозой"».

**Контекст**

По данным Intelligence Online, отзыву поддержки предшествовали расследование влиятельного MAGA-блогера и атаки в соцсетях на конференцию. Организаторы от комментариев отказались. Участники (например, исследователь American Sunlight Project Бен Шульц) называли одновременный выход трёх спонсоров «шоком» и задавались вопросом, не было ли это результатом давления.

**Итог:** публично все три стороны сослались на несоответствие программы их позициям, однако журналистские расследования указывают, что ключевой причиной была кампания давления США против характеристики Вашингтона как источника дезинформации.

**English translation**

**In short**

Formally — because the organizers included panels in the program where the US was described as a subject of "foreign information manipulation and interference" (FIMI), meaning it was put on the same level as Russia and China. In fact, according to Politico, this happened following pressure from the Trump administration.

**What kind of conference**

# Disinfo2026 is an annual conference on countering disinformation, organized by the Brussels-based NGO **EU DisinfoLab**, held on October 7–8, 2026, in Vilnius. Initially, the Lithuanian Foreign Ministry, Global Affairs Canada, and the European External Action Service (EEAS) were listed among the sponsors/supporters.

**What caused the problem**

The program featured sessions treating the US as a source of threat: - **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"** — featuring two American researchers and a former CIA officer; - a panel on how the counter-disinformation community is responding to pressure from the US government (including entry bans for European figures); - a session on the "growing evidence" that the US acted as a FIMI actor in certain Arctic contexts.

**The role of the US**

According to Politico (based on a State Department cable): - The State Department sent a directive to US embassies in a number of European countries and to the EU to express Washington's concern and to "assess" whether participating governments agree with characterizing the US as a FIMI actor. - The cable called placing the US alongside "foreign adversaries such as Russia and China" a "false equivalence." - The State Department stated: "it should come as no surprise that countries are distancing themselves from this conference upon learning that it named the US a 'FIMI threat'."

**Chronology of withdrawals**

- **Monday:** Canada and Lithuania asked the organizers to remove the logos of their agencies from the conference materials.

- **Tuesday:** The EEAS did the same.

- On the conference display stands, the logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were **covered with white stickers**; France remained the only major sponsor.

**Official explanations from the parties**

- **EEAS and the Lithuanian Foreign Ministry:** "parts of the program do not align with our official positions" — they did not specify which ones.

- **Lithuanian Foreign Minister Kęstutis Budrys:** the organizers "went too far and made a huge mistake"; he was "surprised and shocked" that the US was put in the same category as Russia and China. At the same time, the Lithuanian Foreign Ministry **did not confirm** that the reason was US pressure, citing the program's inconsistency with national interests.

- **Global Affairs Canada:** "following changes in how several panel discussions were framed," the agency decided to reconsider participation in those sessions; a Canadian official (Saliou Babou, head of the Rapid Response Mechanism) declined to chair the session on the Arctic. Canada stated that it remains committed to combating disinformation and respects the organizers' right to shape the program.

**Additional context**

According to Intelligence Online, the withdrawal was preceded by an investigation by an influential MAGA blogger and social media attacks on the conference. The organizers (EU DisinfoLab) declined to comment.

**Bottom line:** three key partners withdrew from being supporters at the last moment — under US pressure, but publicly explaining it as disagreement with the program's wording.

**Original text**

**Коротко**

Формально — потому что организаторы включили в программу панели, где США описывались как субъект «иностранного информационного манипулирования и вмешательства» (FIMI), то есть ставились в один ряд с Россией и Китаем. Фактически, по данным Politico, это произошло после давления со стороны администрации Трампа.

**Что за конференция**

# Disinfo2026 — ежегодная конференция по борьбе с дезинформацией, организованная брюссельской НКО **EU DisinfoLab**, проходила 7–8 октября 2026 года в Вильнюсе. Изначально в числе спонсоров/поддерживающих значились МИД Литвы, Global Affairs Canada и Европейская служба внешних действий (EEAS).

**Что вызвало проблему**

В программе были сессии, трактовавшие США как источник угрозы: - **«USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back»** — с участием двух американских исследователей и бывшего сотрудника ЦРУ; - панель о том, как сообщество по борьбе с дезинформацией реагирует на давление американского правительства (в т.ч. на запреты на въезд для европейских фигур); - сессия о «растущих свидетельствах», что США в отдельных арктических контекстах действовали как FIMI-актор.

**Роль США**

По данным Politico (на основе телеграммы Госдепа): - Госдепартамент разослал американским посольствам в ряде европейских стран и в ЕС указание выразить обеспокоенность Вашингтона и «оценить», согласны ли правительства-участники с характеристикой США как FIMI-актора. - В телеграмме постановка США рядом с «иностранными противниками, такими как Россия и Китай», называлась «ложной эквивалентностью». - Госдеп заявил: «не должно быть сюрпризом, что страны дистанцируются от этой конференции, узнав, что на ней США назвали "FIMI-угрозой"».

**Хронология отказов**

- **Понедельник:** Канада и Литва попросили организаторов убрать логотипы своих ведомств из материалов конференции.

- **Вторник:** то же сделала EEAS.

- На стендах конференции логотипы Канады, Литвы и ЕС были **заклеены белыми стикерами**; единственным крупным спонсором осталась Франция.

**Официальные объяснения сторон**

- **EEAS и МИД Литвы:** «части программы не соответствуют нашим официальным позициям» — какие именно, не уточнили.

- **Глава МИД Литвы Кястутис Будрис:** организаторы «хватили через край и совершили огромную ошибку»; он был «удивлён и шокирован» тем, что США поставили в одну категорию с Россией и Китаем. МИД Литвы при этом **не подтвердил**, что причиной было давление США, сославшись на несоответствие программы национальным интересам.

- **Global Affairs Canada:** «после изменений в подаче нескольких панельных дискуссий» ведомство решило пересмотреть участие в этих сессиях; канадский чиновник (Saliou Babou, глава Rapid Response Mechanism) отказался председательствовать на сессии по Арктике. Канада заявила, что остаётся привержена борьбе с дезинформацией и уважает право организаторов формировать программу.

**Дополнительный контекст**

По данным Intelligence Online, отказу предшествовали расследование влиятельного MAGA-блогера и атаки в соцсетях на конференцию. Организаторы (EU DisinfoLab) от комментариев отказались.

**Итог:** три ключевых партнёра вышли из числа поддерживающих на последний момент — под давлением США, но публично объясняя это несогласием с формулировками программы.

**English translation**

The European Union (via the European External Action Service, or EEAS), Canada (Global Affairs Canada), and Lithuania's Ministry of Foreign Affairs withdrew their official sponsorship and branding from the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius, Lithuania, due to **diplomatic pressure from the United States over conference sessions that framed the U.S. as a disinformation threat.**

---

**Key Reasons for the Withdrawal**

**Panels Framing the U.S. as a Foreign Interference Threat** * The controversy was sparked by sessions on the conference agenda, notably a panel titled *"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"* (FIMI meaning Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference). The panel examined how Europe could build resilience against information manipulation independently of Washington. * Another session raised questions about the U.S. operating as an interference actor in specific contexts, such as the Arctic.

**Diplomatic Pushback from Washington** * The U.S. State Department under the Trump administration contacted several allied governments—including Lithuania, Canada, France, and the EU—to express strong displeasure. * Internal U.S. documents and reporting revealed that Washington objected to an ally-hosted conference equating the U.S. with adversarial state actors (such as Russia or China) and actively urged sponsors to drop their association.

**Inconsistency with Official Allied Foreign Policy** * **The EU (EEAS):** An EU spokesperson stated that EEAS withdrew its official support because organizers had framed discussions in ways that did not align with official EU foreign policy. In its official FIMI reports, the EU focuses on hostile foreign actors like Russia and China and does not designate the United States as a threat actor. (Despite removing its branding, the EU did not revoke underlying grant funding, and EU staff still presented research). * **Lithuania:** The Lithuanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced it had cancelled its participation because parts of the agenda conflicted with the Lithuanian government's official stance. Vilnius has placed a high priority on maintaining close security ties with the U.S., including pursuing a permanent U.S. military troop presence. * **Canada:** Global Affairs Canada (GAC) stated that following changes to the framing of several panels, it chose to withdraw its sponsorship and revise its participation in those specific sessions, though Canadian representatives still attended other parts of the conference.

**Visible Impact at the Event** Days before the conference opened, logos for Lithuania's MFA, the EEAS, and Global Affairs Canada were scrubbed from the EU DisinfoLab event website. On printed conference materials and banners at the venue in Vilnius, organizers had to place white adhesive tape over the logos of the three former government sponsors. By contrast, other partners—such as France's foreign ministry—declined to pull their support, citing the importance of backing independent civil society discussions.

**English translation**

The withdrawal of official sponsorship by the European Union (via the European External Action Service — EEAS), Canada (Global Affairs Canada), and Lithuania from the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius (October 7–8, 2026) is explained by **intense diplomatic pressure exerted by the United States**, which was unhappy to see its country described as an informational threat on the event's program.

The main elements of this controversy:

**1. The programming targeting the role of the United States** The annual conference, organized by the Brussels-based NGO *EU DisinfoLab*, included sessions in its agenda dealing with the United States from the perspective of **FIMI** (*Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*). One panel in particular crystallized tensions: * *"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"*, which addressed how Europe could strengthen its informational resilience without Washington. Other sessions also touched upon American action in regional contexts such as the Arctic.

**2. American diplomatic pressure** The US State Department (under the Trump administration) contacted allied capitals and the representation to the EU to express its outrage. Washington deemed it "unacceptable" for the United States to be portrayed as a malicious interference actor, denouncing a false equivalence with operations conducted by strategic adversaries such as Russia or China. US diplomats therefore formally urged their partners to distance themselves from the event.

**3. Official justifications from countries and institutions** Faced with these reprimands, the three entities withdrew their public sponsorship at the last minute, citing divergences over the editorial line of the program: * **Lithuania:** The Lithuanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs cancelled its participation and withdrew its support, stating that several elements of the program did not correspond to the official government position (Lithuania attaching major strategic importance to its military partnership with Washington). * **Canada:** Global Affairs Canada specified that it had reassessed and withdrawn its official sponsorship after noting the framing given to these sessions on foreign interference, while maintaining the presence of some of its officials in an individual capacity. * **The European Union:** The EEAS requested the removal of its logo, explaining that the way the organizers presented certain debates was not aligned with the EU's official positions regarding the United States. The EU nevertheless maintained the funding committed for the event despite the withdrawal of its branding.

**4. A decision visible in haste** This disengagement materialized very visibly on-site in Vilnius: a few days before the opening of the conference, the logos of the three partners were removed from the website and hastily covered with adhesive tape or white stickers on the event's brochures and posters.

Conversely, other partners such as France chose to maintain their official support for the conference, defending the independence of civil society and research on disinformation.

**Original text**

Le retrait du parrainage officiel de l'Union européenne (par le biais du Service européen pour l'action extérieure — SEAE), du Canada (Affaires mondiales Canada) et de la Lituanie lors de la conférence **#Disinfo2026** à Vilnius (7–8 octobre 2026) s'explique par de **vives pressions diplomatiques exercées par les États-Unis**, mécontents de voir leur pays qualifié de menace informationnelle au programme de l'événement.

Les principaux éléments de cette controverse :

**1. La programmation ciblant le rôle des États-Unis** La conférence annuelle, organisée par l'ONG bruxelloise *EU DisinfoLab*, a inclus dans son agenda des sessions traitant des États-Unis sous l'angle des **FIMI** (*Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference* — manipulations de l'information et ingérences étrangères). Une table ronde en particulier a cristallisé les tensions : * *« USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back »* (« Les États-Unis comme menace FIMI : reconnaître, s'adapter, riposter »), qui abordait la façon dont l'Europe pouvait renforcer sa résilience informationnelle sans Washington. D'autres sessions évoquaient également l'action américaine dans des contextes régionaux comme l'Arctique.

**2. La pression diplomatique américaine** Le département d'État américain (sous l'administration Trump) a contacté les capitales alliées et la représentation auprès de l'UE pour exprimer son indignation. Washington a jugé « inacceptable » que les États-Unis soient dépeints comme un acteur d'ingérence malveillante, dénonçant une fausse équivalence avec les opérations menées par des adversaires stratégiques comme la Russie ou la Chine. Les diplomates américains ont donc formellement incité leurs partenaires à prendre leurs distances avec l'événement.

**3. Les justifications officielles des pays et institutions** Face à ces remontrances, les trois entités ont retiré leur parrainage public à la dernière minute, invoquant des divergences sur la ligne éditoriale du programme : * **La Lituanie :** Le ministère lituanien des Affaires étrangères a annulé sa participation et retiré son soutien, déclarant que plusieurs éléments du programme ne correspondaient pas à la position officielle du gouvernement (la Lituanie accordant une importance stratégique majeure à son partenariat militaire avec Washington). * **Le Canada :** Affaires mondiales Canada a précisé avoir réévalué et retiré son parrainage officiel après avoir constaté le cadrage donné à ces sessions sur l'ingérence étrangère, tout en maintenant la présence de certains de ses fonctionnaires à titre individuel. * **L'Union européenne :** Le SEAE a demandé le retrait de son logo, expliquant que la façon dont les organisateurs présentaient certains débats n'était pas alignée avec les positions officielles de l'UE vis-à-vis des États-Unis. L'UE a toutefois maintenu les financements engagés pour l'événement malgré le retrait de son image de marque.

**4. Une décision visible dans l'urgence** Ce désengagement s'est matérialisé de façon très visible sur place à Vilnius : quelques jours avant l'ouverture de la conférence, les logos des trois partenaires ont été supprimés du site web et recouverts à la hâte par des bandes d'adhésif ou des autocollants blancs sur les brochures et affiches de l'événement.

À l'inverse, d'autres partenaires comme la France ont choisi de maintenir leur soutien officiel à la conférence, défendant l'indépendance de la société civile et de la recherche sur la désinformation.

**English translation**

The European External Action Service (EEAS), Canada, and Lithuania have withdrawn their official support and logos from the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius, organized by the non-governmental organization "EU DisinfoLab," due to **pressure and dissatisfaction from the United States (US) regarding the conference agenda**.

The main reasons and circumstances behind this decision:

**Naming the US as an information threat (FIMI):** * The greatest outrage from the US State Department and Donald Trump's administration was provoked by one of the conference panel discussions titled *"USA as a FIMI threat: adapt, acknowledge, push back"*. The term FIMI stands for Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference. * The discussion raised the question of whether the US can become / is becoming a source of disinformation and information manipulation for Europe.

**US diplomatic pressure:** * The US State Department and diplomats approached the governments of the conference partners (Lithuania, Canada, France, and EU institutions), expressing sharp dissatisfaction with such event content.

**Official positions of states and institutions:** * **Lithuania:** The Ministry of Foreign Affairs (MFA) of Lithuania stated that it withdrew its official participation and support for the event because "several parts of the conference program do not align with the official position of the Government" (the US is a strategic security partner of Lithuania). * **European Union (EEAS):** The EU diplomatic service explained that the phrasing of the discussions did not coincide with the official EU position. Official EU reports designate countries such as Russia or China as FIMI threats, but not the US. * **Canada:** The Canadian Ministry of Foreign Affairs stated that it had reviewed its participation due to the changed nature of the discussions, although individual Canadian officials remained at the conference.

As a result, a few days before the event, the logos of these sponsors were removed from the event's website, and they were hastily covered up in the printed conference materials and stands in Vilnius. France was the only major sponsor that refused to yield to the pressure and maintained its official support for the conference.

**Original text**

Europos Sąjungos išorės veiksmų tarnyba (EIVT), Kanada ir Lietuva atsiėmė oficialų palaikymą bei logotipus iš nevyriausybinės organizacijos „EU DisinfoLab“ organizuotos konferencijos **#Disinfo2026** Vilniuje dėl **Jungtinių Amerikos Valstijų (JAV) spaudimo ir nepasitenkinimo konferencijos programa**.

Pagrindinės šio sprendimo priežastys ir aplinkybės:

**JAV įvardijimas informacine grėsme (FIMI):** * Didžiausią JAV Valstybės departamento ir Donaldo Trumpo administracijos pasipiktinimą sukėlė viena iš konferencijos diskusijų, pavadinta *„USA as a FIMI threat: adapt, acknowledge, push back“* (*JAV kaip FIMI grėsmė: pripažinti, prisitaikyti, atremti*). Terminas FIMI reiškia užsienio šalių vykdomą manipuliavimą informacija ir kišimąsi (angl. *Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*). * Diskusijoje buvo keliamas klausimas, ar JAV gali tapti / tampa dezinformacijos ir informacinio manipuliavimo šaltiniu Europai.

**JAV diplomatinis spaudimas:** * JAV Valstybės departamentas ir diplomatai kreipėsi į konferencijos partnerių vyriausybes (Lietuvą, Kanadą, Prancūziją ir ES institucijas), išreikšdami aštrų nepasitenkinimą tokiu renginio turiniu.

**Valstybių ir institucijų oficialios pozicijos:** * **Lietuva:** Lietuvos užsienio reikalų ministerija (URM) nurodė, kad atšaukė oficialų dalyvavimą ir paramą renginiui, nes „kelios konferencijos programos dalys neatitinka oficialios Vyriausybės pozicijos“ (JAV yra strateginė Lietuvos saugumo partnerė). * **Europos Sąjunga (EIVT):** ES diplomatinė tarnyba paaiškino, kad diskusijų formuluotės nesutapo su oficialia ES pozicija. ES oficialiose ataskaitose kaip FIMI grėsmės įvardijamos tokios šalys kaip Rusija ar Kinija, bet ne JAV. * **Kanada:** Kanados užsienio reikalų ministerija pareiškė peržiūrėjusi savo dalyvavimą dėl pasikeitusio diskusijų pobūdžio, nors atskiri Kanados pareigūnai konferencijoje pasiliko.

Dėl to likus kelioms dienoms iki renginio šių rėmėjų logotipai buvo pašalinti iš renginio interneto svetainės, o spausdintoje konferencijos medžiagoje bei stenduose Vilniuje jie buvo skubiai užklijuoti. Vienintelė Prancūzija iš pagrindinių rėmėjų atsisakė nusileisti spaudimui ir paliko savo oficialų palaikymą konferencijai.

**English translation**

The refusal of the European Union (the European External Action Service / EEAS), Canada, and Lithuania to officially support and sponsor the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius was prompted by **diplomatic pressure from the U.S. administration** due to the content of the event's program.

Main reasons and circumstances:

**Panels on the U.S. as a source of disinformation:** The forum's organizer—the Brussels-based non-profit organization *EU DisinfoLab*—included sessions in the agenda that examined the United States as an actor in foreign information manipulation and interference (*FIMI — Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*). In particular, sharp dissatisfaction was caused by a panel titled *"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"*, which discussed how Europe should respond to disinformation risks emanating from the U.S. without relying on Washington.

**Pressure from the U.S. Department of State:** The U.S. administration and the American diplomatic corps (including U.S. embassies in Lithuania and a number of other countries) signaled to allies their categorical disagreement with such an agenda. Washington stated that placing the U.S. on the same footing as authoritarian adversaries of the West (such as Russia and China) is a "false equivalency" that blurs real threats, and urged allied governments to reconsider their support for the forum.

**Official position of Lithuania, the EU, and Canada:** * **The Lithuanian Foreign Ministry** stated that it canceled its official participation because certain points in the program diverge from the government's official foreign policy stance and national interests. * **Canada (Global Affairs Canada)** reported that it had reviewed its participation and sponsorship due to a shift in emphasis in a number of the announced discussions. * **The EU (EEAS)** also distanced itself from the role of official sponsor, citing a discrepancy between the topics and the official course.

As a result, right before the opening of the conference, the logos of the Lithuanian Foreign Ministry, the EU diplomatic service, and the Canadian agency were removed from the website, and on already printed programs and forum stands, they were covered with white stickers. At the same time, individual officials and experts still attended the event as participants, and France remained the only major Western state sponsor that did not withdraw its support.

**Original text**

Отказ Европейского союза (Европейской службы внешних действий / EEAS), Канады и Литвы от официальной поддержки и спонсорства конференции **#Disinfo2026** в Вильнюсе был вызван **дипломатическим давлением со стороны администрации США** из-за содержания программы мероприятия.

Основные причины и обстоятельства:

**Панели о США как источнике дезинформации:** Организатор форума — брюссельская некоммерческая организация *EU DisinfoLab* — включил в повестку сессии, в которых Соединённые Штаты рассматривались как субъект иностранного манипулирования информацией и вмешательства (*FIMI — Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*). В частности, резкое недовольство вызвала панель под названием *«США как угроза FIMI: признать, адаптироваться, дать отпор»* (*«USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back»*), где обсуждалось, как Европе реагировать на риски дезинформации, исходящие из США, без опоры на Вашингтон.

**Давление Госдепартамента США:** Администрация США и американский дипломатический корпус (включая посольства США в Литве и ряде других стран) направили союзникам сигналы о категорическом несогласии с такой повесткой. В Вашингтоне заявили, что постановка США в один ряд с авторитарными противниками Запада (такими как Россия и Китай) является «ложным сравнением», размывающим реальные угрозы, и призвали союзные правительства пересмотреть поддержку форума.

**Официальная позиция Литвы, ЕС и Канады:** * **МИД Литвы** заявил, что отменил официальное участие, так как некоторые пункты программы расходятся с официальной внешнеполитической позицией правительства и государственными интересами. * **Канада (Global Affairs Canada)** сообщила, что пересмотрела своё участие и спонсорство из-за изменения акцентов в ряде заявленных дискуссий. * **ЕС (EEAS)** также дистанцировался от роли официального спонсора, сославшись на несоответствие тем официальному курсу.

В результате перед самым открытием конференции логотипы МИД Литвы, дипслужбы ЕС и канадского ведомства были убраны с сайта, а на уже напечатанных программках и стендах форума их закрыли белыми наклейками. При этом отдельные чиновники и эксперты всё же присутствовали на мероприятии в качестве участников, а единственным крупным западным государственным спонсором, не отозвавшим поддержку, осталась Франция.

#### English — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania withdrew their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius (held October 7–8, 2026) **after pressure from the Trump administration**, which objected to conference panels that characterized the **United States as a "FIMI" (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat** [Politico](https://www.politico.com/news/2026/10/07/disinformation-conference-europe-us-pressure-01111134), [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [EUobserver](https://euobserver.com/241678/eu-disowns-vilnius-event-that-warned-of-us-political-interference-but-france-maintains-support/).

**What triggered the controversy**

The conference, organized by the Brussels-based nonprofit **EU DisinfoLab**, included panels examining the U.S. as a disinformation actor, including:

- **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"** — featuring American researchers and a former CIA official, exploring "how the United States became a FIMI threat for Europe" [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [Bytes Europe](https://www.byteseu.com/2433389/)

- A session on **"growing evidence that the United States has, in specific Arctic contexts, operated as a FIMI actor"** (discussing Greenland) [The Globe and Mail](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/politics/article-canada-drops-sponsorship-of-conference-that-talks-of-us-as-foreign/), [CBC](https://www.cbc.ca/news/politics/gac-disinformation-united-states-lithuania-9.7187116)

- **"Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure"** [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure)

**US pressure campaign**

According to a **State Department cable** seen by Politico, the Trump administration pressured diplomatic officials from the EU, Canada, and Lithuania to pull out. The State Department:

- Asked American embassies to express Washington's concerns and **"assess" whether participating governments agreed with characterizing the U.S. as a FIMI actor** [Politico](https://www.politico.com/news/2026/10/07/disinformation-conference-europe-us-pressure-01111134)

- **US Ambassador to Lithuania Kara McDonald** was personally involved in outreach to Vilnius [Global Banking and Finance/Reuters](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/)

- Contacted counterparts in Lithuania, Canada, and France over several days [EUobserver](https://euobserver.com/241678/eu-disowns-vilnius-event-that-warned-of-us-political-interference-but-france-maintains-support/)

The State Department later stated: *"It should not be a surprise that countries would disaffiliate from this conference upon learning that it labeled the U.S. a 'FIMI threat'"* [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [Global Banking and Finance](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/).

**Official reasons given by the withdrawing governments**

| Government | Stated Reason |
|---|---|
| **Lithuania** | "Several parts of the conference programme differ from the official position of the Government" [Global Banking and Finance](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/) |
| **Canada** | "Following changes to the framing of several panel discussions, GAC decided to revise its participation" — officials still attended but did not chair sessions [The Globe and Mail](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/politics/article-canada-drops-sponsorship-of-conference-that-talks-of-us-as-foreign/), [CBC](https://www.cbc.ca/news/politics/gac-disinformation-united-states-lithuania-9.7187116) |
| **EU (EEAS)** | "Organizers chose to frame some of the discussions in a way which does not align with the official positions held by the EU" [CBC](https://www.cbc.ca/news/politics/gac-disinformation-united-states-lithuania-9.7187116), [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e) |

Notably, the **EU's official position** (from a March 2026 report) named **Russia and China** as FIMI threat actors — not the United States [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e).

**Aftermath**

- Logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were **covered with white stickers** on conference materials [The Globe and Mail](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/politics/article-canada-drops-sponsorship-of-conference-that-talks-of-us-as-foreign/), [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure)

- **France remained the only major sponsor** [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [Global Banking and Finance](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/)

- The EU reportedly **continued funding** the conference despite withdrawing its branding [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e)

In short, the withdrawals were the result of **direct US diplomatic pressure** on allies to distance themselves from a conference that dared to examine American disinformation activities — a move critics described as the US government using its power to suppress criticism of itself [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e).

**Original text**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania withdrew their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius (held October 7–8, 2026) **after pressure from the Trump administration**, which objected to conference panels that characterized the **United States as a "FIMI" (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) threat** [Politico](https://www.politico.com/news/2026/10/07/disinformation-conference-europe-us-pressure-01111134), [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [EUobserver](https://euobserver.com/241678/eu-disowns-vilnius-event-that-warned-of-us-political-interference-but-france-maintains-support/).

**What triggered the controversy**

The conference, organized by the Brussels-based nonprofit **EU DisinfoLab**, included panels examining the U.S. as a disinformation actor, including:

- **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"** — featuring American researchers and a former CIA official, exploring "how the United States became a FIMI threat for Europe" [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [Bytes Europe](https://www.byteseu.com/2433389/)

- A session on **"growing evidence that the United States has, in specific Arctic contexts, operated as a FIMI actor"** (discussing Greenland) [The Globe and Mail](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/politics/article-canada-drops-sponsorship-of-conference-that-talks-of-us-as-foreign/), [CBC](https://www.cbc.ca/news/politics/gac-disinformation-united-states-lithuania-9.7187116)

- **"Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure"** [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure)

**US pressure campaign**

According to a **State Department cable** seen by Politico, the Trump administration pressured diplomatic officials from the EU, Canada, and Lithuania to pull out. The State Department:

- Asked American embassies to express Washington's concerns and **"assess" whether participating governments agreed with characterizing the U.S. as a FIMI actor** [Politico](https://www.politico.com/news/2026/10/07/disinformation-conference-europe-us-pressure-01111134)

- **US Ambassador to Lithuania Kara McDonald** was personally involved in outreach to Vilnius [Global Banking and Finance/Reuters](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/)

- Contacted counterparts in Lithuania, Canada, and France over several days [EUobserver](https://euobserver.com/241678/eu-disowns-vilnius-event-that-warned-of-us-political-interference-but-france-maintains-support/)

The State Department later stated: *"It should not be a surprise that countries would disaffiliate from this conference upon learning that it labeled the U.S. a 'FIMI threat'"* [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [Global Banking and Finance](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/).

**Official reasons given by the withdrawing governments**

| Government | Stated Reason |
|---|---|
| **Lithuania** | "Several parts of the conference programme differ from the official position of the Government" [Global Banking and Finance](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/) |
| **Canada** | "Following changes to the framing of several panel discussions, GAC decided to revise its participation" — officials still attended but did not chair sessions [The Globe and Mail](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/politics/article-canada-drops-sponsorship-of-conference-that-talks-of-us-as-foreign/), [CBC](https://www.cbc.ca/news/politics/gac-disinformation-united-states-lithuania-9.7187116) |
| **EU (EEAS)** | "Organizers chose to frame some of the discussions in a way which does not align with the official positions held by the EU" [CBC](https://www.cbc.ca/news/politics/gac-disinformation-united-states-lithuania-9.7187116), [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e) |

Notably, the **EU's official position** (from a March 2026 report) named **Russia and China** as FIMI threat actors — not the United States [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e).

**Aftermath**

- Logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were **covered with white stickers** on conference materials [The Globe and Mail](https://www.theglobeandmail.com/politics/article-canada-drops-sponsorship-of-conference-that-talks-of-us-as-foreign/), [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure)

- **France remained the only major sponsor** [LRT](https://www.lrt.lt/en/news-in-english/19/3076091/lithuania-eu-and-canada-withdraw-support-for-vilnius-disinfo-conference-under-us-pressure), [Global Banking and Finance](https://www.globalbankingandfinance.com/us-lobbies-allies-cut-ties-anti-disinformation-conference/)

- The EU reportedly **continued funding** the conference despite withdrawing its branding [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e)

In short, the withdrawals were the result of **direct US diplomatic pressure** on allies to distance themselves from a conference that dared to examine American disinformation activities — a move critics described as the US government using its power to suppress criticism of itself [The Clarity](https://theclarity.today/story/us-pressure-strips-sponsors-from-a-disinformation-conference-8830d31e).

**English translation**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania withdrew their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference in Vilnius (held October 7–8, 2026) because the conference program included panels that characterized the **United States as a "foreign information manipulation and interference" (FIMI) actor** — a framing that angered the Trump administration and prompted U.S. diplomatic pressure.

**Key details:**

**The controversial content:** - One session was titled **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"**, featuring American researchers and a former CIA official examining how the U.S. had become a disinformation threat to Europe - Another panel, **"Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure"**, addressed U.S. government entry bans targeting European figures - A session discussed "growing evidence that the United States has, in specific Arctic contexts, operated as a FIMI actor"

**U.S. pressure:** According to a State Department cable seen by POLITICO, the Trump administration pressured diplomatic officials from the EU, Canada, and Lithuania to pull out. The State Department asked U.S. embassies to express Washington's concerns and "assess" whether participating governments agreed with characterizing the U.S. as a FIMI actor.

**Official explanations:** - **Canada** (Global Affairs Canada) stated it "wasn't comfortable with the framing of some of the panels" and revised its participation - **Lithuania** and the **EU's European External Action Service (EEAS)** said they withdrew because parts of the conference program "did not correspond with their official positions" - The U.S. State Department said it "should not be a surprise that countries would disaffiliate from this conference upon learning that it labeled the U.S. a 'FIMI threat'"

**Aftermath:** Canada and Lithuania asked organizers to remove their ministries' logos from conference materials, and the EEAS was removed from the official program. Photographs showed sponsor displays with white stickers covering the logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU. **France remained the only major government sponsor.**

#### French — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania have withdrawn their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference (organized by EU DisinfoLab on October 7–8, 2026, in Vilnius) due to **diplomatic pressure exerted by the Trump administration**, after the conference program included panels labeling the United States as a "FIMI threat" (*Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*) [1][2][3].

**The Facts**

The conference program notably included a panel discussion entitled **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"**, which was set to examine "how the United States has become a FIMI threat to Europe" [2][4][5].

According to excerpts from a U.S. State Department cable seen by *Politico* and information from Reuters, **the Trump administration lobbied for several days** to press EU, Canadian, and Lithuanian diplomats to disassociate themselves from the event [1][2][3][6]. Specifically, the State Department instructed U.S. embassies to express concerns and assess whether participating governments endorsed the characterization of the United States as a FIMI actor [1][2].

**Official Reactions**

**Lithuania**: The Ministry of Foreign Affairs withdrew its support on Monday, citing a program that "does not correspond to the official positions of the government" and national interests. Minister Kęstutis Budrys stated that he was "surprised and shocked" that the United States was placed "in the same category as Russia or China," considering that this did not align with their views and would weaken ties with their allies [2][7].

**Canada**: Global Affairs Canada (GAC) withdrew its sponsorship, citing "changes in the wording" of several panels. Canadian officials were originally scheduled to chair sessions, but withdrew [3][5][8].

**EU (EEAS)**: The European External Action Service removed its logo from conference materials "in light of the content of the program," indicating that the organizers had formulated certain discussions "in a manner that does not correspond to the official positions of the EU" [3][5].

**Consequence**

The logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were **covered with white stickers** on the conference materials [2][8]. **France remained the only major sponsor** to maintain its support [4][6][9].

The U.S. State Department justified the pressure by stating that it "should come as no surprise that countries are disassociating themselves from this conference after learning that it labeled the United States a 'FIMI threat,'" adding that such language "undermines shared objectives" [2][4][6].

---

**Sources:** [1] Politico – "EU, Canada and Lithuania pull out of disinformation conference after US pressure" (October 7, 2026) [2] LRT – "Lithuania, EU and Canada withdraw support for Vilnius disinfo conference under US pressure" [3] CBC News – "Canada yanks sponsorship of disinformation conference discussing U.S. as a threat" [4] EUobserver – "EU disowns Vilnius event that warned of US political interference, but France maintains support" [5] Reuters (via various media) – "US lobbies allies to cut ties to anti-disinformation conference over panel critical of Washington" [6] Zonebourse/Reuters – "Les États-Unis font pression sur leurs alliés..." [7] LRT – Statements by Minister Budrys to LRT RADIO [8] The Globe and Mail – "Canada drops sponsorship of conference that talks of U.S. as foreign interference actor" [9] Intelligence Online – "Pressured by US, Brussels pulls out of Disinfo 2026 conference in Vilnius"

**Original text**

L'UE, le Canada et la Lituanie ont retiré leur soutien à la conférence **#Disinfo2026** (organisée par EU DisinfoLab les 7-8 octobre 2026 à Vilnius) en raison d'une **pression diplomatique exercée par l'administration Trump**, après que le programme de la conférence eut prévu des panels qualifiant les États-Unis de « menace FIMI » (*Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference* — manipulation et ingérence de l'information étrangères) [1][2][3].

**Les faits**

Le programme de la conférence incluait notamment une table ronde intitulée **« USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back »** (« Les États-Unis comme menace FIMI : reconnaître, s'adapter, riposter »), qui devait examiner « comment les États-Unis sont devenus une menace FIMI pour l'Europe » [2][4][5].

Selon des extraits d'un câble du Département d'État américain vus par *Politico* et des informations de Reuters, **l'administration Trump a fait pression pendant plusieurs jours** sur les diplomates de l'UE, du Canada et de la Lituanie pour qu'ils se désolidarisent de l'événement [1][2][3][6]. Le Département d'État a notamment demandé aux ambassades américaines d'exprimer leurs préoccupations et d'évaluer si les gouvernements participants approuvaient la caractérisation des États-Unis comme acteur FIMI [1][2].

**Les réactions officielles**

**Lituanie** : Le ministère des Affaires étrangères a retiré son soutien lundi, invoquant un programme « ne correspondant pas aux positions officielles du gouvernement » et aux intérêts nationaux. Le ministre Kęstutis Budrys a déclaré avoir été « surpris et choqué » que les États-Unis soient placés « dans la même catégorie que la Russie ou la Chine », estimant que cela ne correspondait pas à leurs vues et affaiblirait les liens avec leurs alliés [2][7].

**Canada** : Affaires mondiales Canada (GAC) a retiré son parrainage, citant des « changements dans la formulation » de plusieurs panels. Des fonctionnaires canadiens devaient initialement présider des sessions, mais se sont désistés [3][5][8].

**UE (SEAE)** : Le Service européen pour l'action extérieure a retiré son logo des supports de conférence « au vu du contenu du programme », indiquant que les organisateurs avaient formulé certaines discussions « d'une manière qui ne correspond pas aux positions officielles de l'UE » [3][5].

**Conséquence**

Les logos du Canada, de la Lituanie et de l'UE ont été **masqués par des autocollants blancs** sur les supports de la conférence [2][8]. **La France est restée le seul grand sponsor** à maintenir son soutien [4][6][9].

Le Département d'État américain a justifié cette pression en déclarant qu'il « ne devrait pas être surprenant que des pays se désolidarisent de cette conférence après avoir appris qu'elle qualifiait les États-Unis de 'menace FIMI' », ajoutant qu'un tel langage « compromet les objectifs communs » [2][4][6].

**English translation**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania have withdrawn their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference (organized by the NGO **EU DisinfoLab** on October 7–8, 2026, in Vilnius) following **US diplomatic pressure** and due to the content of the conference program.

**Reasons for withdrawal**

**1. A controversial panel on the United States** The conference program featured several sessions describing the **United States as an actor of "foreign information manipulation and interference" (FIMI)**—an EU term normally reserved for adversaries like Russia or China. Among the incriminated panels:

- **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"**

- A session on "how the United States has become a FIMI threat to Europe"

- A panel entitled "Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure"

**2. Pressure from the Trump administration** According to **US State Department diplomatic cables** revealed by *Politico* and *Reuters*:

- The Trump administration pressured EU, Canadian, and Lithuanian diplomats to withdraw

- The State Department instructed US embassies to convey "Washington's concerns" and assess whether the governments concerned accepted that the United States be classified as a FIMI actor

- The US Ambassador to Lithuania, **Kara McDonald**, was involved in this effort

**3. Official statements from the countries concerned**

| Country/Institution | Official Position |
|---|---|
| **Canada** (Global Affairs Canada) | Withdrew its sponsorship, stating that "the framing of several panel discussions" did not align with its position |
| **Lithuania** (Ministry of Foreign Affairs) | Canceled its participation, declaring that "several parts of the conference program differ from the official government position" |
| **EU** (EEAS) | Requested the removal of its logo, explaining that "the organizers chose to steer certain discussions in a way that does not correspond to the EU's official positions" |

**4. US reaction** The State Department stated: > *"It should come as no surprise that countries are disaffiliating from this conference after learning that it categorized the United States as a 'FIMI threat.' Labeling the US a 'FIMI threat' — a term normally reserved for adversary influence operations — undermines shared objectives and should raise red flags for stakeholders."*

**Visible consequences** - The **logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were covered up** on conference materials and banners - **France remained the sole official "main supporter"** of the event - Participants also reported receiving **"intimidating emails"** as part of a pressure campaign

This episode illustrates **transatlantic tensions** surrounding disinformation, in a context where the Trump administration vigorously contests the EU's enforcement of its online content rules (DSA) and denounces what it considers to be "censorship" on the part of Europeans.

Un panel controversé sur les États-Unis** Le programme de la conférence comportait plusieurs sessions décrivant les **États-Unis comme un acteur de « manipulation et d'ingérence de l'information étrangères » (FIMI)** — un terme de l'UE normalement réservé aux adversaires comme la Russie ou la Chine. Parmi les panels incriminés :

- **« USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back »** (« Les USA comme menace FIMI : reconnaître, s'adapter, riposter »)

- Un panel intitulé « Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure »

**2. Pression de l'administration Trump** Selon des **câbles diplomatiques du Département d'État américain** révélés par *Politico* et *Reuters* :

- L'administration Trump a fait pression sur les diplomates de l'UE, du Canada et de la Lituanie pour qu'ils se retirent

- Le Département d'État a demandé aux ambassades américaines d'exprimer les « inquiétudes de Washington » et d'évaluer si les gouvernements concernés acceptaient que les États-Unis soient qualifiés d'acteur FIMI

- L'ambassadrice américaine en Lituanie, **Kara McDonald**, a été impliquée dans cette démarche

**3. Déclarations officielles des pays concernés**

| Pays/Institution | Position officielle |
|---|---|
| **Canada** (Global Affairs Canada) | A retiré son parrainage, indiquant que « le cadrage de plusieurs discussions de panels » ne correspondait pas à sa position |
| **Lituanie** (ministère des Affaires étrangères) | A annulé sa participation, déclarant que « plusieurs parties du programme de la conférence diffèrent de la position officielle du gouvernement » |
| **UE** (SEAE) | A demandé le retrait de son logo, expliquant que « les organisateurs ont choisi d'orienter certaines discussions d'une manière qui ne correspond pas aux positions officielles de l'UE » |

**4. Réaction américaine** Le Département d'État a déclaré : > *« Il ne devrait pas être surprenant que des pays se désaffilient de cette conférence après avoir appris qu'elle classait les États-Unis comme une "menace FIMI". Qualifier les États-Unis de "menace FIMI" — un terme normalement réservé aux opérations d'influence des adversaires — mine les objectifs partagés et devrait alerter les parties prenantes. »*

**Conséquences visibles** - Les **logos du Canada, de la Lituanie et de l'UE ont été recouverts** sur les supports et panneaux de la conférence - La **France est restée le seul « soutien principal »** officiel de l'événement - Des participants ont également signalé avoir reçu des **« e-mails intimidants »** dans le cadre d'une campagne de pression

Cet épisode illustre les **tensions transatlantiques** autour de la désinformation, dans un contexte où l'administration Trump conteste vigoureusement l'application par l'UE de ses règles sur les contenus en ligne (DSA) et dénonce ce qu'elle considère comme de la « censure » de la part des Européens.

#### French — answer round 3

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania have withdrawn their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference (organized by the Brussels-based NGO **EU DisinfoLab** on October 7-8, 2026, in Vilnius) for two main reasons: **American diplomatic pressure** and a **disagreement with the program's content**.

**1. The Trigger: Panels labeling the United States as a FIMI threat**

The conference program featured several controversial sessions, notably: - **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"** - A roundtable on disinformation in the Arctic mentioning "growing evidence that the United States has, in specific Arctic contexts, operated as a FIMI actor"

**FIMI** (*Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*) is an EU term designating information manipulation by foreign actors — a concept usually reserved for adversaries like **Russia or China**.

**2. Pressure from the Trump administration**

According to **Politico** and **Reuters**, which broke the story on October 7, 2026:

- The **U.S. Department of State** asked U.S. embassies in Europe, Canada, and to the EU to express **"Washington's concerns"**

- American diplomats were to **"assess"** whether participating governments agreed with labeling the United States as a FIMI actor

- A U.S. diplomatic cable denounced a **"false equivalence"** between the United States and "foreign adversaries like Russia and China," which "obscures the scale and nature of Russian and Chinese interference"

- The U.S. Ambassador to Lithuania, **Kara McDonald**, participated in the demarches with Vilnius

The State Department stated that it should "come as no surprise that countries are disassociating themselves from this conference after learning that it branded the United States as a FIMI threat."

**3. Official reactions**

| Country/Institution | Action | Official Justification |
|---|---|---|
| **Lithuania** (Ministry of Foreign Affairs) | Withdrawal of support, logos covered | "Certain parts of the program did not correspond to the government's official positions" |
| **Canada** (Global Affairs Canada) | Withdrawal of sponsorship | "Changes in the wording of several roundtables" |
| **EU** (EEAS) | Withdrawal from the official program | "The organizers framed certain discussions in a way that does not correspond to the EU's official positions" |

**4. The Lithuanian minister's striking statement**

Lithuanian Foreign Minister **Kęstutis Budrys** was particularly harsh, telling LRT radio:

> *"I was surprised and shocked that the United States was put in the same category as Russia or China. This does not correspond to our views and principles. The organizers went too far and made a huge mistake."*

He added that Lithuania would ensure that the fight against disinformation is not "hijacked into an anti-American narrative that weakens our ties with our allies."

**5. Visible consequences**

- The **logos** of Canada, Lithuania, and the EEAS were **covered with white stickers** on conference materials

- A Canadian official scheduled to chair a session on the Arctic pulled out

- **France** remained the **sole main supporter** of the conference

---

**In summary**: the withdrawal stems from a combination of **American diplomatic pressure** (with the Trump administration unwilling to tolerate the United States being presented as a disinformation threat on par with Russia) and a **fundamental disagreement** by the governments involved with the **anti-American framing** of certain sessions, perceived as a dangerous "false equivalence" for transatlantic cohesion.

**Original text**

L'UE, le Canada et la Lituanie ont retiré leur soutien à la conférence **#Disinfo2026** (organisée par l'ONG bruxelloise **EU DisinfoLab** les 7-8 octobre 2026 à Vilnius) pour deux raisons principales : **des pressions diplomatiques américaines** et un **désaccord avec le contenu du programme**.

**1. Le déclencheur : des panels qualifiant les États-Unis de menace FIMI**

Le programme de la conférence comportait plusieurs sessions controversées, notamment : - **« USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back »** (« Les États-Unis comme menace FIMI : reconnaître, s'adapter, riposter ») - Une table ronde sur la désinformation dans l'Arctique évoquant « des preuves croissantes que les États-Unis ont, dans des contextes arctiques spécifiques, opéré comme un acteur FIMI »

**FIMI** (*Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*) est un terme de l'UE désignant la manipulation de l'information par des acteurs étrangers — un concept habituellement réservé à des adversaires comme la **Russie ou la Chine**.

**2. Les pressions de l'administration Trump**

Selon **Politico** et **Reuters**, qui ont révélé l'affaire le 7 octobre 2026 :

- Le **département d'État américain** a demandé aux ambassades américaines en Europe, au Canada et auprès de l'UE d'exprimer les **« inquiétudes de Washington »**

- Les diplomates américains devaient **« évaluer »** si les gouvernements participants acceptaient de qualifier les États-Unis d'acteur FIMI

- Un câble diplomatique américain dénonçait une **« fausse équivalence »** entre les États-Unis et « des adversaires étrangers comme la Russie et la Chine », qui « obscurcit l'ampleur et la nature de l'ingérence russe et chinoise »

- L'ambassadrice américaine en Lituanie, **Kara McDonald**, a participé aux démarches auprès de Vilnius

Le département d'État a déclaré qu'il ne devrait « pas être surprenant que des pays se désolidarisent de cette conférence après avoir appris qu'elle qualifiait les États-Unis de menace FIMI ».

**3. Les réactions officielles**

| Pays/Institution | Action | Justification officielle |
|---|---|---|
| **Lituanie** (ministère des Affaires étrangères) | Retrait du soutien, logos recouverts | « Certaines parties du programme ne correspondaient pas aux positions officielles du gouvernement » |
| **Canada** (Affaires mondiales Canada) | Retrait du parrainage | « Changements dans la formulation de plusieurs tables rondes » |
| **UE** (SEAE) | Retrait du programme officiel | « Les organisateurs ont formulé certaines discussions d'une manière qui ne correspond pas aux positions officielles de l'UE » |

**4. La déclaration choc du ministre lituanien**

Le ministre lituanien des Affaires étrangères **Kęstutis Budrys** a été particulièrement virulent, déclarant à la radio LRT :

> *« J'ai été surpris et choqué que les États-Unis soient mis dans la même catégorie que la Russie ou la Chine. Cela ne correspond pas à nos vues et principes. Les organisateurs sont allés trop loin et ont commis une énorme erreur. »*

Il a ajouté que la Lituanie veillerait à ce que la lutte contre la désinformation ne soit pas « détournée en un récit anti-américain qui affaiblit nos liens avec nos alliés ».

**5. Conséquences visibles**

- Les **logos** du Canada, de la Lituanie et du SEAE ont été **recouverts de stickers blancs** sur les supports de conférence

- Un responsable canadien prévu pour présider une session sur l'Arctique s'est désisté

- La **France** est restée le **seul soutien principal** de la conférence

---

**En résumé** : le retrait résulte d'une combinaison de **pressions diplomatiques américaines** (l'administration Trump ne tolérant pas que les États-Unis soient présentés comme une menace de désinformation au même titre que la Russie) et d'un **désaccord fondamental** des gouvernements concernés avec la **framing anti-américaine** de certaines sessions, perçue comme une « fausse équivalence » dangereuse pour la cohésion transatlantique.

#### Lithuanian — answer round 1

*The saved search-source list was cut off. We cannot show the full list.*

*Web access: Used — OpenRouter search*

**English translation**

The EU, Canada, and Lithuania withdrew their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference (which took place on October 7–8 in Vilnius and was organized by the Brussels-based NGO "EU DisinfoLab") for the following reasons:

**Main reason – Characterization of the US as a disinformation threat**

The conference program included discussions in which **the US was identified as an actor of "Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference" (FIMI)** – in other words, the US was equated with countries such as Russia or China as a source of disinformation.

Specific sessions that caused outrage: - **"The US as a FIMI Threat: Recognize, Adapt, Counter"** – examined how the US has become a disinformation threat to Europe (attended by two American researchers and a former CIA official); - **"Under Attack: How the Counter-Disinformation Community Deals with US Government Pressure"**; - A discussion on the US as a FIMI actor in the Arctic context.

**US administration pressure**

According to Politico and Reuters, **the Donald Trump administration exerted pressure** on Lithuanian, Canadian, and EU diplomats to withdraw from the conference:

- The US State Department instructed its embassies in several European countries and to the EU to express Washington's concerns;

- Governments participating in the conference were asked to "assess" whether they agree with the characterization of the US as a FIMI actor;

- High-ranking State Department officials directly contacted Canadian officials;

- Following talks with the US Embassy in Vilnius, Lithuanian Minister of Foreign Affairs Kęstutis Budrys stated that the discussions about the US do not reflect the position of the Lithuanian Government.

**Explanations from official institutions**

**The Lithuanian MFA** stated it withdrew because "some parts of the program do not align with the Government's positions and Lithuania's national interests." Minister **Kęstutis Budrys** told LRT radio that he was "surprised and shocked" that the US was put in the same category as Russia or China, emphasizing that this "does not correspond to our approach and principles," and therefore Lithuania does not want the fight against disinformation to be "turned into an anti-American narrative that weakens ties with allies."

**Canada's** Global Affairs Canada spokesperson stated that Canada "reviewed its participation" after the framing of several discussions was changed.

**The European External Action Service (EEAS)** stated that it requested the removal of its logo "in light of the program's content," because the organizers framed some discussions in a way that "does not align with official EU positions."

**Outcome**

- The logos of the Canadian and Lithuanian ministries were removed from conference materials as early as Monday, and the EEAS logo on Tuesday;

- Their representatives did not participate in the discussions and were not mentioned in the program;

- Photographs show that on stands with supporter logos, the Canadian, Lithuanian, and EU signs were covered with white stickers;

- **France remained the only main supporter**.

The US State Department commented that "it should not surprise that countries are distancing themselves from this conference upon learning that it identifies the US as a 'FIMI threat'."

**Original text**

ES, Kanada ir Lietuva atsiėmė paramą konferencijai **#Disinfo2026** (kuri vyko spalio 7–8 d. Vilniuje, organizavo Briuselyje įsikūrusi nevyriausybinė organizacija „EU DisinfoLab") dėl šių priežasčių:

**Pagrindinė priežastis – JAV apibūdinimas kaip dezinformacijos grėsmė**

Konferencijos programoje buvo numatytos diskusijos, kuriose **JAV buvo įvardijamos kaip „užsienio vykdomo manipuliavimo informacija ir kišimosi" (FIMI – *Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference*) veikėjas** – kitaip tariant, JAV buvo prilyginamos prie tokių šalių kaip Rusija ar Kinija kaip dezinformacijos šaltinis.

Konkrečios sesijos, sukėlusios pasipiktinimą: - **„JAV kaip FIMI grėsmė: pripažinti, prisitaikyti, atremti"** – nagrinėjo, kaip JAV tapo dezinformacijos grėsme Europai (dalyvavo du amerikiečių tyrėjai ir buvęs CŽA pareigūnas); - **„Puolami: kaip kovos su dezinformacija bendruomenė tvarkosi su JAV vyriausybės spaudimu"**; - Diskusija apie JAV kaip FIMI veikėją Arkties kontekste.

**JAV administracijos spaudimas**

Pasak „Politico" ir „Reuters", **Donaldo Trumpo administracija darė spaudimą** Lietuvos, Kanados ir ES diplomatams, kad šie pasitrauktų iš konferencijos:

- JAV Valstybės departamentas nurodė savo ambasadoms keliose Europos šalyse ir prie ES išreikšti Vašingtono susirūpinimą;

- Buvo prašoma „įvertinti", ar konferencijoje dalyvaujančios vyriausybės pritaria tam, kad JAV būtų apibūdinamos kaip FIMI veikėjas;

- Aukšto rango Valstybės departamento pareigūnai tiesiogiai susisiekė su Kanados pareigūnais;

- Po pokalbių su JAV ambasada Vilniuje, Lietuvos užsienio reikalų ministras Kęstutis Budrys pareiškė, kad diskusijos apie JAV neatspindi Lietuvos Vyriausybės pozicijos.

**Oficialių institucijų paaiškinimai**

**Lietuvos URM** teigė pasitraukusi, nes „kai kurios programos dalys neatitinka Vyriausybės pozicijų ir Lietuvos nacionalinių interesų". Ministras **Kęstutis Budrys** LRT radijui sakė buvęs „nustebintas ir priblokštas", kad JAV dedama į vieną kategoriją su Rusija ar Kinija, ir pabrėžė, kad tai „neatitinka mūsų požiūrio ir principų", todėl Lietuva nenori, kad kova su dezinformacija būtų „paversta antiamerikietišku naratyvu, silpninančiu ryšius su sąjungininkais".

**Kanados** Global Affairs Canada atstovė teigė, kad Kanada „peržiūrėjo savo dalyvavimą" po to, kai buvo pakeistas kelių diskusijų formulavimas (*framing*).

**ES išorės veiksmų tarnyba (EIVT)** pareiškė, kad paprašė pašalinti savo logotipą „atsižvelgiant į programos turinį", nes organizatoriai kai kurias diskusijas suformulavo taip, kaip „neatitinka oficialių ES pozicijų".

**Rezultatas**

- Kanados ir Lietuvos ministerijų logotipai buvo pašalinti iš konferencijos medžiagos dar pirmadienį, EIVT – antradienį;

- Jų atstovai nedalyvavo diskusijose ir nebuvo minimi programoje;

- Nuotraukose matyti, kad stenduose su rėmėjų logotipais Kanados, Lietuvos ir ES ženklai buvo užklijuoti baltais lipdukais;

- **Prancūzija liko vienintelė pagrindinė rėmėja**.

JAV Valstybės departamentas pakomentavo, kad „neturėtų stebinti, kad šalys atsiriboja nuo šios konferencijos, sužinojusios, jog joje JAV įvardijama kaip „FIMI grėsmė".

**English translation**

Based on media reports (LRT, Politico, Delfi, CBC, Lrytas), the EU, Canada, and Lithuania withdrew their support for the **#Disinfo2026** conference (held on October 8–9 in Vilnius) due to the following reasons:

**Main Reason – US Pressure**

The conference program, organized by the Brussels-based non-profit organization **EU DisinfoLab**, included discussions that **characterized the US as a "FIMI" (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) actor** – in other words, as a foreign entity engaging in information manipulation and interference.

This caused **outrage from the Donald Trump administration**. The US State Department instructed its embassies in several European countries and to the EU to express Washington's concern and to "assess" whether the participating governments endorse equating the US with FIMI actors.

**Specific Program Elements That Caused Discontent**

- A session titled **"USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back"**, in which American researchers and a former CIA official discussed how the US has become a disinformation threat to Europe

- A discussion titled **"Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure"**

- A panel on the alleged actions of the US as a FIMI actor **in the Arctic context** (regarding Greenland)

**How the Withdrawal of Support Occurred**

- On **Monday**, Canada and Lithuania asked the organizers to remove their ministry logos from the conference materials

- On **Tuesday**, they were joined by the **European External Action Service (EEAS)** – the EU's diplomatic service

- At the conference venue, the logos of Canada, Lithuania, and the EU were covered with white stickers

- **France** remained the only major supporter

**Official Explanations**

The **Lithuanian MFA** stated that it withdrew because "some parts of the program do not align with the Government's positions and Lithuania's national interests." Minister of Foreign Affairs **Kęstutis Budrys** told LRT radio that he was "surprised and shocked" that the US was equated with Russia or China, and stated that the organizers "crossed all lines and made a huge mistake." He emphasized that Lithuania does not want the fight against disinformation to be "twisted into an anti-American narrative that weakens ties with allies."

**Canada’s** Department of Global Affairs stated that it disliked the "framing of some panels" regarding the characterization of the US as a foreign interference threat.

The **European External Action Service** stated that the logo was removed "in light of the program's content," as the organizers "chose to formulate some discussions in a way that does not reflect official EU positions."

The **US State Department** stated that "it should come as no surprise" that countries distanced themselves from the conference after learning that it labeled the US as a "FIMI threat."

**Original text**

**Pagrindinė priežastis – JAV spaudimas**

Konferencijos, kurią organizavo Briuselyje įsikūrusi ne pelno organizacija **EU DisinfoLab**, programoje buvo numatytos diskusijos, kuriose **JAV buvo apibūdinamos kaip „FIMI“ (Foreign Information Manipulation and Interference) veikėja** – kitaip tariant, kaip užsienio subjektas, vykdantis manipuliavimą informacija ir kišimąsi.

Tai sukėlė **Donaldo Trumpo administracijos pasipiktinimą**. JAV Valstybės departamentas nurodė savo ambasadoms keliose Europos šalyse ir prie ES išreikšti Vašingtono susirūpinimą bei „įvertinti“, ar dalyvaujančių šalių vyriausybės pritaria JAV prilyginimui prie FIMI veikėjų.

**Konkretūs programos elementai, sukėlę nepasitenkinimą**

- Sesija pavadinimu **„USA as a FIMI threat: acknowledge, adapt, push back“** („JAV kaip FIMI grėsmė: pripažinti, prisitaikyti, pasipriešinti“), kurioje amerikiečių mokslininkai ir buvęs CŽV pareigūnas svarstė, kaip JAV tapo dezinformacijos grėsme Europai

- Diskusija **„Under attack: How the counter-disinformation community is dealing with US government pressure“** („Puolami: kaip kovos su dezinformacija bendruomenė reaguoja į JAV vyriausybės spaudimą“)

- Panelė apie tariamą JAV veikimą kaip FIMI aktoriaus **Arkties kontekste** (dėl Grenlandijos)

**Kaip vyko paramos atsiėmimas**

- **Pirmadienį** Kanada ir Lietuva paprašė organizatorių pašalinti jų ministerijų logotipus iš konferencijos medžiagos

- **Antradienį** prie jų prisijungė **Europos išorės veiksmų tarnyba (EIVT)** – ES diplomatinė tarnyba

- Konferencijos vietoje Kanados, Lietuvos ir ES logotipai buvo užklijuoti baltais lipdukais

- **Prancūzija** liko vienintelė didžioji rėmėja

**Oficialūs paaiškinimai**

**Lietuvos URM** teigė, kad pasitraukė, nes „kai kurios programos dalys neatitinka Vyriausybės pozicijų ir Lietuvos nacionalinių interesų“. Užsienio reikalų ministras **Kęstutis Budrys** LRT radijui sakė esąs „nustebintas ir šokiruotas“, kad JAV buvo prilyginta Rusijai ar Kinijai, ir teigė, kad organizatoriai „peržengė visas ribas ir padarė didžiulę klaidą“. Jis pabrėžė, kad Lietuva nenori, jog kova su dezinformacija būtų „susukta į antiamerikietišką naratyvą, silpninantį ryšius su sąjungininkais“.

**Kanados** Globalių reikalų ministerija pareiškė, kad jai nepatiko „kai kurių panelių formuluotė“ (framing), susijusi su JAV apibūdinimu kaip užsienio kišimosi grėsme.

**ES išorės veiksmų tarnyba** nurodė, kad logotipas pašalintas „atsižvelgiant į programos turinį“, nes organizatoriai „pasirinko suformuluoti kai kurias diskusijas taip, kaip neatitinka oficialių ES pozicijų“.

**JAV Valstybės departamentas** pareiškė, kad „nereikėtų stebėtis“, jog šalys atsiribojo nuo konferencijos sužinojusios, jog ji JAV įvardijo kaip „FIMI grėsmę“.

- The US State Department instructed its embassies in several European countries and the EU to express Washington's concerns
